Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you're misinterpreting these comments and messages?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it’s possible in theory, but it really doesn’t feel that way to me. It feels pretty clear that those comments and Slack messages are meant as signals, like they’re pointing me toward certain career opportunities. I know it sounds unusual, but I’m pretty convinced they’re coordinating it quietly.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that your classmates and the startup advisor are coordinating opportunities for you?\n\nPatient: About two months now. It started around when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program, while I was still working my operations job.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other thoughts that you're convinced are true, but that others might find hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Mostly that same thing, honestly. I’m really convinced the classmate and the startup advisor are communicating indirectly with me, like through comments in class or Slack messages. It feels like they’re sending coded signals about what opportunities I’m supposed to pursue, even though I know other people might think that sounds hard to believe.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other thoughts like this, where you're convinced of something that others might not believe?\n\nPatient: Not really anything separate from that. It’s mostly this belief that my MBA classmate and the startup advisor are kind of coordinating things quietly for me. Like, certain comments in class or Slack messages feel coded, like they’re pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. I know other people might not believe that, but I’ve been pretty convinced of it.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts about your classmate and the startup advisor coordinating opportunities for you ever changed or wavered since you first started believing it?\n\nPatient: It’s wavered a little in the sense that I know it sounds hard to explain, and I’ll question how it would look to someone else. But most of the time I’m still convinced it’s happening — like the comments in class or Slack messages feel too specific, like they’re coded signals about where I’m supposed to go next.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been convinced of anything else that you thought was true, but others might not believe, outside of this situation with your classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: No, not outside of that situation. It’s really been focused on them — my classmate and the startup advisor — and this feeling that they’re quietly setting things up or sending me signals through class comments or Slack. I know it sounds hard to explain, but that’s the main thing I’ve been convinced of.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the comments or messages from your classmate and the startup advisor are specifically meant for you, or could they be intended for someone else?\n\nPatient: I believe they’re meant for me. I know that probably sounds strange, but when my classmate says certain things in class or the advisor posts something in Slack, it feels too specific to just be general. Like they’re signaling what I’m supposed to do next with my career.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other thoughts that you were completely convinced of, but that might seem strange or unlikely to others?\n\nPatient: Not really other things, no. The main thing I’ve been completely convinced of is this situation with my MBA classmate and the startup advisor — that they’re kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
